<!-- page 1 -->
NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, May 9, 2017
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, May 9, 2017, in the Supervisors’
Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, Holbrook, Arizona. The Board may vote to meet in a closed executive
session to discuss certain matters as indicated on the following agenda. The Board may meet in executive session pursuant to
A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3) for legal advice on any item.
Persons with disabilities who need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of the Board’s office
at (928) 524-4053; at least 48 hours prior to the meeting so an accommodation can be arranged.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, with the exception of items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at Navajo
County website, www.navajocountyaz.gov 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up to
5 minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any
comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be
placed on a future agenda.
 
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in nature
and will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any item to be
removed from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes: April 25, 2017  
 
b. Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000, April 2017  
 
c. Clerk of Superior Court Report April 2017  
 
d. Juvenile Probation Report March and April 2017  
 
e. Justice Court Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1, March & April; Winslow Precinct #2, March &
April; Snowflake Precinct #3, March & April; Kayenta Precinct #4, March & April; Show Low
Precinct #5, March & April; and Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct #6, March & April, 2017 
 
 
f. Application for supervised fireworks display permit submitted by the Heber-Overgaard
Chamber of Commerce
 
 
g. Contracts signed by County Manager pursuant to Board of Supervisors Resolution: United
States Department of the Interior Bureau of Land Management Amended Right of Way Grant
Serial Number AZA 19232
 
 
h. Sympathy letter to the family of Renita Lupe, Whiteriver Librarian

<!-- page 2 -->
i. Letter to Honorable Jeff Flake regarding support for the Payment in Lieu of Taxes (PILT)
Program
 
 
j. Keynote Contract with Elizabeth Smart for the FY18 Victims Rights Symposium  
 
3. BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
 
a. Presentation to the Snowflake Junior High School STEAM participants for winning the
Samsung Solve for Tomorrow Contest on April 26, 2017, in New York. 
 
Presented By: Supervisor Jason Whiting
 
b. Recognition to White Mountain Apache Head Start for receiving the Four-Star Quality Plus
rating through Arizona's First Things First Quality First program.
 
Presented By: Supervisor Dawnafe Whitesinger/Jessica Beach
 
c. Consideration of a Proclamation declaring May 14th - May 20th, 2017 as Random Acts of
Kindness Week. 
 
Presented By: Ron Smith
 
4. SHERIFF'S OFFICE:  
 
a. Consideration of a Proclamation declaring May 7-13 "Correctional Officers Week".  
Presented By: Sheriff K.C. Clark
 
5. ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES:  
 
a. Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions.
Presented By: Katherine Wright
 
 
b. Presentation to retiring employee Chester Robinson.  
Presented By: Katherine Wright/Glenn Kephart
 
c. Budget Update.
Presented By: Bryan Layton
 
 
6. COUNTY ATTORNEY:  
 
a. Consideration and acceptance of FY18 DPS VOCA Victim Assistance Discretionary Training
Grant.
 
Presented By: Lynda Young
 
7. TREASURER:  
 
a. Consideration of approving abatement of personal property taxes.  
Presented By: Debra Kester
 
8. NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors

<!-- page 3 -->
8. NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors
Session: 
 
 
a. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board wishes
to discuss a particular item: 
Presented By: Health Department Director, Jeff Lee
 
 
1. Agreement between The Arizona Early Childhood Development and Health Board Navajo
Nation Regional Partnership Council (First Things First) and Navajo County Public Health
Services District to provide nutrition education in the Navajo Nation region. 
 
 
2. Intergovernmental Agreement with the Arizona Department of Health Services, Contract
Number ADHS17-161985 for HIV Prevention Program.  
 
 
3. Amendment Number 5 to the Intergovernmental Agreement with the Arizona Department of
Health Services, Agreement Number ADHS15-094982 for the Healthy People Healthy
Communities program. 
 
 
9. PROBATION:  
 
a. Presentation for partnership with NCIS.  
Presented By: Arno Hall/Jalyn Gerlich
 
10. PUBLIC WORKS:  
 
a. Consideration of Amendment No. 2 to the Contract for Hauling Waste Tires.  
Presented By: Glenn Kephart, P.E.
 
11. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from Board members, County Attorney and County
Manager.
 
 
 
12. ADJOURN  
 
 
Dated: May 4, 2017 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted: 11:30 a.m. By: MB                         
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.i.        
Meeting Date: 05/09/2017  
Title: PILT Support Letter 2017
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Board of Supervisors
Motion before the Board:
Letter to Honorable Jeff Flake regarding support for the Payment in Lieu of Taxes (PILT) Program
Background:
PILT is a critical piece of our county budget and failure to reauthorize these funds will
imperil our
county’s ability to fulfill our statutorily-mandated functions, serving the public interest.
Attachments
PILT Support Letter 2017

Final Approval Date: 05/02/2017

<!-- page 419 -->
• 928.524.4053 • Fax: 928.524.4239 • P.O. Box 668 • Holbrook, AZ 86025 •  
• www.navajocountyaz.gov • 
  
NAVAJO COUNTY  
Board of Supervisors 
 
 
  
     Lee Jack Sr. • Jesse Thompson •Jason E. Whiting • Steve Williams • Dawnafe Whitesinger  
“We are Navajo County” 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
May 1, 2017 
 
 
 
Honorable Jeff Flake 
2200 East Camelback Rd. 
Suite 120 
Phoenix, AZ 85016 
 
Dear Senator Flake, 
 
I write to you today on behalf of the Navajo County Board of Supervisors to thank you for your 
continued support of the full-funding for the Payment in Lieu of Taxes Program (PILT) .  The 
sustainability of PILT, including a permanent full funding solution  is essential for counties such 
as Navajo that are hugely impacted by large amount of federally managed land.  
 
The current uncertain status of PILT funding, designed to partially offset the lost revenue and 
additional cost for services, including law enforcement, search and rescue, emergency services and 
road maintenance associated with the large amount of tax-exempt federal public lands is troubling.  
PILT is a critical piece of our county budget and failure to reauthorize these funds will imperil our 
county’s ability to fulfill our statutorily-mandated functions, serving the public interest. 
 
Thank you again for your understanding of the issue and support of finding a real solution for PILT 
funding.  It is our hope that the Energy and Natural Resources Committee, along with the full 
Senate, the House and President will work together in a bi -partisan manner to find a fair and 
dependable solution for PILT funding. 
 
Appreciate you and your service to our State. 
 
Respectfully, 
 
Jesse Thompson, Chairman 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors

Manager
Department: County Attorney
Motion before the Board:
Keynote Contract with Elizabeth Smart for the FY18 Victims Rights Symposium
Background:
Human Trafficking and predatory crimes touch Navajo County daily.  We believe that by
partnering with Elizabeth Smart and her message, we will be able to have an impact serving
victims of these crimes.

Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: $10,500.00
Fiscal Impact:

remaining 65% will be donations.

<!-- page 422 -->
Terms & Conditions  
 Parties to the Agreement:  ​ The Parties to this agreement are First Class Talent (“FCT”) and  ​ Navajo County Attorney's  
 Office ​  (“Talent Buyer”).  Both Signatories warrant that they are duly authorized to enter into this agreement on behalf of  
 their respective Organizations. FCT is the booking agent for the Speaker, however the Speaker is an independent  
 contractor and in exclusive control over the means, method & details of fulfilling the services outlined in the contract.  
 FCT is not responsible for any act or omission of the Speaker. Neither party to this agreement is the agent of the other  
 or in any kind of joint venture or partnership. As this contract is for personal services, the agreement is not assignable  
 to any other party without prior written approval from the other party.    
  
 Dispute Resolution:  ​ The payment terms of this contract, as listed on page 1, are of essence to this contract. The failure  
 to pay according to this schedule is considered material breach of the contract, and an acceptance of the fee by FCT  
 after this date is not to be considered a waiver. Any conflict or dispute of this agreement shall be adjudicated by  
 binding arbitration by a single arbitrator in accordance with the Rules for Commercial Arbitration of the American  
 Arbitration Association at the location of Franklin, TN. The arbitrator, to be mutually selected by the parties, shall have  
 the power to award damages, costs and attorneys’ fees in his/her discretion and subject to the principles of equity. The  
 Talent Buyer agrees to reimburse FCT for any expenses incurred collecting any and/or all of the payments due under  
 this contract. This contract is to be construed under Tennessee Law, and the parties submit to the Jurisdiction of  
 Williamson County, TN.    
  
 Cancellation Terms when Talent Buyer Cancels:  ​ In the event that the Talent Buyer fails to abide by the payment terms  
 or there is another material breach of the contract, Talent Buyer will receive notice of the breach and have five (5) days  
 to cure the same; if it does not cure such breach, the Speaker will be informed of the Breach and the Speaker will not  
 be required to speak at  the contracted event. In the event of any cancellation initiated by the Talent Buyer for any  
 reason, including due to a material breach, Talent Buyer agrees to surrender the deposit if the cancellation is made in  
 advance of sixty (60) days of the event. The Talent Buyer agrees to surrender the entire amount if the cancellation is  
 made within sixty (60)  days of the contracted date.    
  
 Cancellation Terms when Speaker/Performer Cancels:  ​ The Speaker may cancel the agreement for the following  
 reasons with thirty (30) days’ prior written notice to Talent Buyer (except in the case of bona fide illness, force majeure  
 or death of an immediate relative of Speaker, where reasonable prior written notice must be given) if Speaker learns of  
 information relating to the Talent Buyer’s organization or event that creates a substantial Public Relations or Conflict of  
 Interest issue. In the event the Speaker cancels for one of these reasons, the Talent Buyer will be reimbursed their  
 money (once refunded to FCT by the Speaker) if FCT cannot provide the Talent Buyer with another Speaker which is  
 acceptable to the Talent Buyer. Damages for cancellation under any circumstance are limited to the refund of the  
 money already paid to FCT. No consequential damages or anything similar are allowed for under this contract.  
  
 Indemnification:  ​ Speaker shall indemnify and pay the costs of defense of Talent Buyer and each of their respective  
 officers, directors, employees, volunteers, members and agents (Indemnified Parties) and Speaker shall hold  
 Indemnified Parties harmless from and against any and all injuries, losses, threats of loss, claims, actions, damages,  
 liabilities and expenses (including without limitation, attorney fees and expenses) arising from, related to or in  
 connection with any acts or omissions by Speaker at the Event, including but not limited to infringement of a third  
 party’s intellectual property, defamation, loss, damage, injury or death suffered by a third party as a result of the  
 Speaker’s negligence, willful misconduct or a breach of this Agreement. Indemnified Parties shall not be liable or  
 responsible for, and Speaker hereby releases Indemnified Parties from, all liability or responsibility to Speaker or any  
 person claiming by, through or under Speaker, by way of subrogation or otherwise, for any injury, loss or damage to any  
 person or property in or around the Property, or to Speaker’s business or services or products irrespective of the cause  
 of such injury, loss or damage.  
  
 Talent Buyer shall indemnify and pay the costs of defense of Speaker and each of their respective officers, directors,  
 employees, volunteers, members and agents (Indemnified Parties) and Talent Buyer shall hold Indemnified Parties  
 harmless from and against any and all injuries, losses, threats of loss, claims, actions, damages, liabilities and expenses  
 (including without limitation, attorney fees and expenses) arising from, related to or in connection with any acts or  
 omissions by Talent Buyer at the Event, including but not limited to infringement of a third party’s intellectual property,  
 defamation, loss, damage, injury or death suffered by a third party as a result of Talent Buyer’ negligence, or a breach  
 of this Agreement. Indemnified Parties shall not be liable or responsible for, and Talent Buyer hereby releases  
 Indemnified Parties from, all liability or responsibility to Talent Buyer or any person claiming by, through or under Talent  
 Buyer, by way of subrogation or otherwise, for any injury, loss or damage to any person or property in or around the  
 Property, or to Talent Buyer business or services or products irrespective of the cause of such injury, loss or damage.  
  
 Taxes & Fees:  ​ The Talent Buyer shall pay any and all Federal or State Amusement or similar taxes.  These are taxes  
 that would require the Event Buyer to “withhold” a portion of the agreed honorarium from either the deposit or balance,  
 resulting in a reduced fee payable to the Speaker. Notwithstanding the preceding sentence, each party shall be  
 Contract Number: B231062

<!-- page 423 -->
responsible for their own U.S. income taxes.    
 Expenses:  ​ Unless Parties agree to “Flat Fee Travel”, the Talent Buyer shall be responsible for all actual, reasonable,  
 customary and non-marked up travel expenses (for example Airfares, Car Service, Rental Cars, Hotel, Travel Meals etc),  
 in addition to honorarium payment(s) for the Engagement , as set forth in the Invitation.  Hotel accommodation shall be  
 “on-site” at the event venue.    
  
 Venue & Event Requirements:  ​ The Talent Buyer shall provide a well-heated (or cooled as the case may be) well-lit, safe  
 and appropriate Venue for the Engagement. All equipment and facilities shall be in good working condition together  
 with the necessary stage, accessories and properties including without limitation, microphones and amplification  
 system. The Talent Buyer shall abide by all building code requirements. No additional appearances or activities shall be  
 planned by the Talent Buyer or Talent Buyer nor expected of the Speaker unless expressly contained as part of the  
 terms of this Agreement. The fee listed is understood to be for the Engagement (i.e., the speech or other performance)  
 only; any additional activities must be negotiated independently with FCT and set forth in writing. Any material change  
 in the nature of the Engagement shall constitute a breach of this Agreement. In the event that the Speaker has any  
 specific requirements, including without limitation, the need for any special equipment, any such requirements shall be  
 provided in writing and shall be attached to this Agreement as an Addendum.  
  
 Confidentiality:  ​ Each party agrees to maintain the absolute confidentiality of all the terms, conditions, and  
 arrangements contained in the contract and/or associated with the appearance by Speaker, except that Talent Buyer  
 may publicize Speaker’s appearance and presentation at the Event after the Invitation and Speaker’s  
 acknowledgement have been executed. Any breach of this confidentiality shall be deemed a material breach of this  
 Agreement for which the breaching Party shall be held liable for any resulting damages.  
  
 Force Majeure:  ​ Notwithstanding any other provision of this Agreement, in the event that the performance of any  
 obligation under this Agreement by either Party is prevented due to acts of God, exchange controls, export or import  
 controls, or any other government restriction, wars, hostilities, blockades, civil disturbances, revolutions, strikes, terrorist  
 attacks, lockouts, or any other cause beyond the reasonable control of a Party, such Party shall not be responsible to  
 the other Parties for failure or delay in performance of its obligations under this Agreement. Each Party shall promptly  
 notify the other Parties of such force majeure condition and reschedule the event for a time after the Force Majeure  
 occurrence has ended.  If the Event cannot be rescheduled, Speaker or FCT, as applicable, will fully refund Talent  
 Buyer.  
  
 Recording Prohibited ​ : It is agreed that the Speaker’s address, may not be recorded by any means, including without  
 limitation, on audiotape, videotape or film, nor may it be broadcast or streamed on the Internet, in whole or in part,  
 without the prior written permission of Bureau. All materials provided by the speaker are protected by Copyright Laws  
 of the USA.  
  
 Separability:  ​ Should any provision of this Agreement be held to be void, invalid or inoperative, the remaining provisions  
 of this Agreement shall not be affected and shall continue in effect and the invalid provision shall be deemed modified  
 to the least degree necessary to remedy such invalidity.     
  
 Interpretation:  ​ This Agreement has been entered into after review and negotiation of its terms by the Parties hereto,  
 who have both had the opportunity to be represented by counsel. The Agreement shall be fairly interpreted in  
 accordance with its terms and without any strict construction in favor of or against either Party on grounds that this  
 Agreement or any provision thereof was drafted or proposed by such Party.    
  
 Entire Agreement:  ​ This Agreement sets forth the entire understanding between the Parties; it shall become effective  
 when a fully executed copy is received by FCT. This Agreement may not be altered, changed, modified or waived in  
 whole or part except by another agreement in writing signed by both Parties. An executed facsimile copy, digital  
 execution or photocopy of this contract shall be deemed an original.    
  
 Miscellaneous: ​  Speaker may have an event rider. Talent buyer acknowledges that they understand that the rider (if  
 applicable) is part of this agreement.  
 Itinerary/Schedule listed on page one (1) of this agreement is of essence to this agreement.  Any amendment to the  
 schedule must be agreed to in writing by both parties.

that research proves helps children thrive, such as training for teachers to expand their skills and to
help create learning environments that nurture the emotional, social and academic development of
every child.

Consideration of a Proclamation declaring May 14th - May 20th, 2017 as Random Acts of

Background: 
The Navajo County CARE Committee's mission is to assist Navajo County employees. The
CARE Committee assists employees during family tragedies, illness etc. The CARE Committee
would like to increase morale within the Navajo County family.

Form Review

<!-- page 427 -->
Navajo County               
Proclamation 
 
“May 14th – May 20th, 2017 as Random Act of Kindness Week” 
  
WHEREAS, each and every day at Navajo County Complexes, Road Yards, Sub Stations, 
etc., Random Acts of Kindness are performed by the employees of Navajo County; and 
WHEREAS, each act of kindness makes for a higher morale among Navajo County 
employees and inspires more acts of kindness towards others; and 
WHEREAS, many of these Random Acts of Kindness are done anonymousl y thereby 
creating feelings of goodwill all around us; and 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County CARE Committee participates each month by picking 6 
names from the birthday list and delivering a mug with assorted candies to sweeten the 
employees month; and 
WHEREAS, in many locations across the County these Random Acts of Kindness have 
been appreciated with positive feedback being shared among others. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT PROCLAIMED , by the Board of Supervisors of Navajo 
County, that the week of May 1 4th – May 20th, 2017, is designated as Random Acts of 
Kindness Week and to encourage the employees of Navajo County, to join the Board of 
Supervisors and the Navajo County CARE Committee in sharing kindness throughout our 
work locations and beyond. 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Board of Supervisors on May 9, 2017. 
    
           ____________________________ 
Jesse Thompson, Chairman

_________________________________ 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 4.a.        
Meeting Date: 05/09/2017  
Title: Correctional Officers Week Proclamation
Submitted By: Donna Hurstrom, Deputy Clerk of the Board
Department: Board of Supervisors
Presented By: Sheriff K.C. Clark
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of a Proclamation declaring May 7-13 "Correctional Officers Week". 
Background:
Correctional officers and employees are capable, committed, patient and persistent professionals.
They serve admirably in correctional facilities all across the country as chaplains, teachers,
counselors, supervisors, managers and directors. They help keep our citizens and communities
safe. Correctional officers and employees teach, train, mentor, preach and cure. Though the job of
correctional officers and employees is often very challenging and, at times, stressful, the
thousands who work the corrections profession every day perform their duties well and with great
pride. They are brave, courageous and tenacious individuals who put their lives and health at risk
every day to keep the public safe and provide a multitude of services to the nation’s offenders.
They have a tremendous responsibility and are owed our nation’s gratitude. Correctional officers
are most deserving of our respect, our thanks and the highest praise. They are well-trained, always
on watch and prepared to meet any challenge. The men and women working in corrections make a
difference in our communities. On behalf of the American Correctional Association and our
thousands of members in the U.S. and internationally, we commend all those who currently serve
in the corrections profession, those who have served and those who have given their lives in
pursuit of this most honorable profession. 
Recommendation: 
Approval

Proclamation

<!-- page 429 -->
Navajo County               
Proclamation 
CORRECTIONAL OFFICERS WEEK 
WHEREAS, Navajo County’s correctional officers are trained law enforcement professionals dedicated to 
maintaining safe correctional facilities and ensuring the public safety; and 
WHEREAS, correctional facilities across the U.S., both civilian and military, public and private, adult and 
juvenile, are run by highly qualified and experienced individuals with a deep understanding of the challenges 
within the profession; and 
WHEREAS, correctional officers and employees are responsible for the custody, care and rehabilitation of 
thousands of offenders every year, as well as the maintenance of safe and secure facilities; and 
 
WHEREAS, correctional officers and employ ees are resourceful, capable, committed, patient and persistent 
professionals in criminal justice and our nation; and 
 
WHEREAS, correctional officers and employees serve admirably in many different capacities, including jail 
administrators, wardens, chaplains, nurses, supervisors, social workers, teachers, managers and directors; and 
 
WHEREAS, correctional officers and employees teach, mentor, counsel and treat thousands of offenders; and 
 
WHEREAS, correctional officers and employees provide offenders with direction, hope and a new focus while 
assisting with reentry and life outside corrections; and 
 
WHEREAS, correctional officers and employees rise to meet any challenge and serve this honorable professional 
nobly and admirably; and  
 
WHEREAS, correctional o fficers and employees deserve the recognition for their service and should be 
commended by leaders in the profession, the public and our elected officials for the tremendous job they do and 
exceptional performance of duties under the most difficult of circumstances. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT PROCLAIMED, by the Board of Supervisors of Navajo County, that May 7-13, 
2017 is designated as “Correctional Officers Week”. 
 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Board of Supervisors on May 9, 2017. 
 
         ____________________________ 
Jesse Thompson, Chairman 
 
ATTEST: 
_________________________________ 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Board

Title: FY18 DPS VOCA Victim Assistance Discretionary Training Grant
Submitted For: Brad Carlyon, County Attorney  Submitted By: Lynda Young, Office
Manager
Department: County Attorney
Presented By: Lynda Young
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and acceptance of FY18 DPS VOCA Victim Assistance Discretionary Training
Grant.
Background:
Through VOCA funds, the Arizona Department of Public Safety has awarded Navajo County
grant funds to bring targeted training to the victim service providers within our County. 
These funds will pay for quarterly trainings to service providers.  We will bring in subject matter
experts to provide training on topics that are pertinent to providing quality service to victims. 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval and acceptance of grant agreement.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2018
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 12,719
Fiscal Impact:
This grant is 100% funded through VOCA funds with no match requirement.  $0 impact to General
Fund.

U.S. Department of Justice 
Office of Justice Programs 
Office of the Chief Financial Officer 
 
Certifications Regarding Lobbying; Debarment,  
Suspension and Other Responsibility Matters 
(Dated 7/15/2015) 
 
Applicants should refer to the regulations cited below to determine the certification to which they 
are required to attest. Applicants should also review the instructions for certification included in 
the regulations before completing this form. Acceptance of this form provides for compliance 
with certification requirements under 28 CFR Part 69, "New Restrictions on Lobbying," 2 CFR 
Part 2867, "DOJ Implementation of OMB Guidance on Nonprocurement Debarment and 
Suspension," and 28 CFR Part 83, "Government-wide Debarment and Suspension."  
 
The certifications shall be treated as a material representation of fact upon which reliance will be 
placed when the Department of Justice determines to award the covered transaction, grant, or 
cooperative agreement.  
 
Certification Regarding Lobbying  
As required by Section 1352, Title 31 of the U.S. Code, and implemented at 28 CFR Part 69, for 
persons entering into a grant or cooperative agreement over $100,000, as defined at 28 CFR 
Part 69, the applicant certifies that:  
 
(a) No Federal appropriated funds have been paid or will be paid, by or on behalf of the 
undersigned, to any person for influencing or attempting to influence an officer or employee 
of any agency, a Member of Congress, an officer or employee of Congress, or an employee 
of a Member of Congress in connection with the making of any Federal grant, the entering 
into of any cooperative agreement, and the extension, continuation, renewal, amendment, or 
modification of any Federal grant or cooperative agreement;  
 
(b) If any funds other than Federal appropriated funds have been paid or will be paid to any 
person for influencing or attempting to influence an officer or employee of any agency, a 
Member of Congress, an officer or employee of Congress, or an employee of a Member of 
Congress in connection with this Federal grant or cooperative agreement, the undersigned 
shall complete and submit Standard Form - LLL, "Disclosure of Lobbying Activities," in 
accordance with its instructions;  
 
(c) The undersigned shall require that the language of this certification be included in the 
award documents for all subawards at all tiers (including subgrants, contracts under grants 
and cooperative agreements, and subcontracts) and that all sub-recipients shall certify and 
disclose accordingly.  
 
Certification Regarding Debarment, Suspension and Other Responsibility Matters  
Pursuant to Executive Order 12549, Debarment and Suspension, implemented at 2 CFR Part 
2867, for prospective participants in primary covered transactions, as defined at 2 CFR Section 
2867.20(a), and other requirements:  
 
A. The applicant certifies that it and its principals:  
(a) Are not presently debarred, suspended, proposed for debarment, declared ineligible, 
sentenced to a denial of Federal benefits by a State or Federal court, or voluntarily excluded 
from covered transactions by any Federal department or agency;

<!-- page 456 -->
(b) Have not within a three-year period preceding this application been convicted of or had a 
civil judgment rendered against them for commission of fraud or a criminal offense in 
connection with obtaining, attempting to obtain, or performing a public (Federal, State, or 
local) transaction or contract under a public transaction; violation of Federal or State 
antitrust statutes or commission of embezzlement, theft, forgery, bribery, falsification or 
destruction of records, making false statements, or receiving stolen property; 
  
(c) Have not within a two-year period preceding this application been convicted of a felony 
criminal violation under any Federal law, unless such felony criminal conviction has been 
disclosed in writing to the Office of Justice Programs (OJP) at 
Ojpcompliancereporting@usdoj.gov, and, after such disclosure, the applicant has received a 
specific written determination from OJP that neither suspension nor debarment of the 
applicant is necessary to protect the interests of the Government in this case. 
  
(d) Are not presently indicted for or otherwise criminally or civilly charged by a governmental 
entity (Federal, State, or local) with commission of any of the offenses enumerated in 
paragraph (b) of this certification; and  
 
(e) Have not within a three-year period preceding this application had one or more public 
transactions (Federal, State, or local) terminated for cause or default.  
 
B. Where the applicant is unable to certify to any of the statements in this certification, he or she 
shall attach an explanation to this application. 
  
Federal Taxes 
A. If the applicant is a corporation, the applicant certifies that either (1) the corporation has no 
unpaid Federal tax liability that has been assessed, for which all judicial and administrative 
remedies have been exhausted or have lapsed, that is not being paid in a timely manner 
pursuant to an agreement with the authority responsible for collecting the tax liability, or (2) the 
corporation has provided written notice of such an unpaid tax liability (or liabilities) to OJP at 
Ojpcompliancereporting@usdoj.gov, and, after such disclosure, the applicant has received a 
specific written determination from OJP that neither suspension nor debarment of the applicant 
is necessary to protect the interests of the Government in this case.  
 
B. Where the applicant is unable to certify to any of the statements in this certification, he or she 
shall attach an explanation to this application. 
 
As the duly authorized representative of the applicant, I hereby certify that the applicant will 
comply with the above certification.

Presented By: Debra Kester
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of approving abatement of personal property taxes.
Background:
Per ARS 42-19118 - Clearing uncollectible tax. Also, ARS 42-18353 - Certificate of removal and
abatement; purging record of tax, penalty and interest.
These tax bills have been researched by the Sheriff's Office, Assessor's Office and the Treasurer's
Office that led to an unsuccessful attempt to try and locate owner and property.
Please refer to attached spread sheet.
Recommendation: 
Remove from tax roll.

Board of Supervisors Regular 8.a.1.        
Meeting Date: 05/09/2017  
Title: FTF Navajo Nation Nutrition Contract Renewal
Submitted For: Jeffrey Lee, Health Director 
Submitted By: Janelle Linn, Nurse Supervisor
Department: NC Public Health Services District
Motion before the Board:
Agreement between The Arizona Early Childhood Development and Health Board Navajo Nation
Regional Partnership Council (First Things First) and Navajo County Public Health Services
District to provide nutrition education in the Navajo Nation region. 
Background:
Recommend approving the Government Agreement.  This is a contract through First Things First,
and would be a renewal of the contract we've had with them for the past three years. The intent of
this evidence based nutrition, obesity and physical activity strategy is to provide nutrition
education to 2000 children birth through 5 years of age, and 4000 adults in the Navajo Nation
region, particularly as part of a comprehensive and coordinated system.  The expected results are
the reduction in the prevalence of obesity rates within children and their family members, in order
to prevent lifelong complications to health and wellbeing.
The term of this agreement is July 1, 2017 through June 30, 2018. 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2017
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: $582,930
Fiscal Impact:
This is a grant agreement.  The contract allows $582,930 to complete the deliverable outlined in the
agreement.  This is not a matching grant, and is self-sustaining, allowing for a solvent program.
Attachments
GRA-RC001-18-0884-01

Board of Supervisors Regular 8.a.2.        
Meeting Date: 05/09/2017  
Title: HIV IGA ADHS17-161985
Submitted By: Janelle Linn, Nurse Supervisor
Department: NC Public Health Services District
Motion before the Board:
Intergovernmental Agreement with the Arizona Department of Health Services, Contract Number
ADHS17-161985 for HIV Prevention Program.  
Background:
This contract has been in place with Arizona Department of Health Services HIV Prevention
Program for 20+ years.  This would be a renewal of that contract to perform HIV prevention
activities throughout our community.The purpose of this grant contract is to allow Navajo County
to provide HIV Prevention activities to our community.  Goals of the program include: 
Providing high quality free or reduced price HIV testing, and linkage to care for HIV
positive and high risk HIV individuals
Increase the number of individuals in our jurisdiction who aware of their HIV status
To bring all area providers in line with National Goals, and Funding Opportunity
Announcement
Provide partner services in public and private sectors to all persons newly diagnosed with
HIV, previously positive with a new STD diagnosis, and to all HIV positive persons or their
medical providers requesting continuing partner services. 
It also allows for HIV surveillance activities, which is a responsibility that is required by Arizona
Revised Statute.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2017
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 32,770.00
Fiscal Impact:
This is a non-matching, cost reimbursement grant.  The entire budget is for $32,770.  Half of that
amount ($16,385) is able to be used during this contract cycle (7-1-17 to 21-31-17).

Board of Supervisors Regular 8.a.3.        
Meeting Date: 05/09/2017  
Title: HPHC IGA Amendment Agreement No ADHS15-094982
Submitted For: Jeffrey Lee, Health Director 
Submitted By: Janelle Linn, Nurse Supervisor
Department: NC Public Health Services District
Motion before the Board:
Amendment Number 5 to the Intergovernmental Agreement with the Arizona Department of
Health Services, Agreement Number ADHS15-094982 for the Healthy People Healthy
Communities program. 
Background:
Recommend approval of this contract amendment.  It's doesn't change the contract deliverables, or
original total budget award amount.This amendment is to adjust purchase orders, change orders
and to revise and replace the original contract price sheet.  The original total award amount is not
changing, just the line item totals.  This grant encompasses the Health District's Family Planning,
Injury Prevention, Tobacco, Teen Pregnancy Prevention, HAPI and Accreditation Programs.  It
allows us to group various grant funding to reduce the number of contracts, grouping them into
one.  Funding allows the Health District to provide a variety of essential health services/education
throughout our community.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2016
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: $476,279
Fiscal Impact:
The original contract award amount of $476,279 is not changing.  The funding for the Children
With Special Healthcare Needs had it's own line item, and is now being grouped in the Health in
Arizona Policy Initiative Line Item of Funding.  This will effect purchase orders and change orders
as well.  This contract is a cost reimbursement contract, and in non-matching.

<!-- page 539 -->
Where do the juveniles go? 
•ARS  15-308. Providing educational services of an 
accommodation school -  
•Navajo County Accommodation District has an 
At Risk High School

<!-- page 540 -->
What does a typical day look like? 
 
8:00 am – Traditional School Day starts 
1:30 pm – School is dismissed 
 
1:30- 5:00 – Extended Learning Opportunities such as: 
Counseling, Life Skills, Leadership, Tutoring, iCivics,          
                    Citizenship, Workforce Readiness Skills, Physical 
        Conditioning and endless possibilities 
 
Dismissal – To current home site with electronic monitoring.

<!-- page 541 -->
In Conclusion 
• No added cost to the county – redirection of funds  
 
• Probation will be housed in the facility. 
 
• Space to provide instruction in life skills, after the regular school day. 
 
• Targeted youth would be receiving the treatment to meet their needs. 
 
• Transportation to the school will be provided.

Meeting Date: 05/09/2017  
Title: Approval of Amendment No. 2 to the Contract for Hauling Waste Tires
Submitted For: Glenn Kephart, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Jeanine Carruthers, Deputy Director Administration
Department: Public Works
Presented By: Glenn Kephart, P.E.
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of Amendment No. 2 to the Contract for Hauling Waste Tires.
Background:
On June 9, 2015, the Board of Supervisors approved the Contract with CRM of America, LLC
(CRM) for the Hauling of Waste Tires, Contract #B15-04-027.  CRM was the only bid received
and this contract has worked very well for the County.  This Contract may be renewed for up to
four additional one-year terms; therefore, we are recommending that the contract be extended one
additional year (June 9, 2017 to June 8, 2018).
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval.
